The *raḥmah* — mercy — is the word that the commentators identify as the *prophethood* itself, in the sense of being the *gift* of the *office*, in the sense of being the *bestowal* of the *message*, in the sense of being the *granting* of the *capacity* to *carry* the *burhān*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *word*, that the *burhān* is *not* the *prophet's* *achievement*; it is God's *mercy*, in the *sense of being* *granted*, in the *sense of being *bestowed*, in the *sense of being* *given* *without* *earning*. The *sūrah* is *answering*, in the *clause*, the *notables'* *third* *objection* — the *lack of merit* — by *restating* the *source*: the *merit* is *not* the *prophet's*; it is God's *mercy*, and the *mercy* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables'* *search* for *merit* in the *prophet* is a *search* in the *wrong* *place*, and the *wrong place* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *merit* is *in the source*, not in the *channel*.

**"And You Are Blind to It" — The Diagnosis That the Sūrah Gives**

The third clause is the *diagnosis*: *wa-antum la-hā ʿumy* — "and you are blind to it." The *ʿumy* — blindness — is the *word* that verse 24 *used* for the *first* *group* in the *parable*, and the *use* here is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables'* *refusal* is *not* a *refusal* of the *obscure*; it is a *blindness* to the *clear*, and the *blindness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *burhān* is *present* and *clear*, and that the *notables* *cannot* *see* it, and that the *cannot* is *not* a *defect* of the *burhān*; it is a *condition* of the *notables*, and the *condition* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *diagnosis* of verse 20 — the *spiritual* *blindness* — is *in operation* in *Noah's* *notables*, and that the *operation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *blindness* is the *same* *blindness*, and the *sameness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mirror* is *complete*.

**"Shall We Force It Upon You?" — The Principle That the Sūrah Establishes**

The verse closes with the *principle*: *afanajburakum wa-antum kāhirūn* — "shall we force it upon you, though you are unwilling?" The *question* is *rhetorical*, and the *answer* is *no*, and the *no* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* the *principle* that the *Qur'an* states in 2:256:

> *"There is no compulsion in religion. The right course has become clear from the wrong."*

The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *closing* *question*, that the *prophet's* *office* is *not* an *office of force*; it is an *office of presentation*, and the *presentation* is *complete* when the *proof* is *stated*, and the *statement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *choice* is the *people's*, and the *choice* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *not* *coerced*; it is *chosen*, and the *choosing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *verdict* is *just*, because the *one* *verdicted* was *not* *forced*, and the *not being forced* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *refusal* is *free*, and the *freeness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *real*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:29

> **O my people! I ask not of you any wealth in return for it; my reward lies only with God. And I shall not drive away those who believe; truly they shall meet their Lord. But I see that you are an ignorant people.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"I Ask Not of You Any Wealth"**

The verse records Noah's answer to his people: *wa-yā qawmi lā asʾalukum ʿalayhi mālan* — "O my people! I ask not of you any wealth in return for it." The commentators explain that the *it* in *in return for it* refers to what Noah was calling his people to, namely faith in and sincere worship of the One God.

Al-Ṭabarī's gloss fixes the referent. The refusal of payment is not a general statement about prophecy but a specific denial that the message is being sold. The commentators read this as answering an unspoken suspicion.

The word *māl* — wealth, property — is concrete. The commentators note that Noah does not refuse an abstract reward but money, which is what the notables would have understood as the motive for a man gathering followers.

The clause *in ajriya illā ʿalā Llāh* — "my reward lies only with God" — completes the statement. The commentators read the two halves together: no payment from the people, and a reward from God that does not depend on their acceptance.

This is a recurring feature of the Qur'an's prophets. The commentators note the same formula in the mouths of Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, Shuʿayb, and the Prophet himself, and read the recurrence as establishing that the message is not a transaction.

**"I Shall Not Drive Away Those Who Believe"**

The verse's central refusal is *wa-lā aṭṭaridu lladhīna āmanū* — "and I shall not drive away those who believe." The commentators supply the occasion.

Al-Ṭabarī records that the notables to whom Noah brought the message told him that if he wanted them to believe in him, he should shun those who were currently following him, because they were unworthy of being on equal footing with the notables. The commentators connect this to v. 27, where the notables describe the followers as the lowest among them.

The request is a condition of belief, and Noah's refusal is unconditional. The commentators note that he does not negotiate the terms or propose a compromise; he states that he will not do it.

The commentators observe that the Quraysh had the same issue with the Prophet's followers, and direct the reader to 6:52c. The parallel makes the verse a pattern rather than an isolated incident.

The ground of the refusal follows: *innahum mulāqū rabbihim* — "truly they shall meet their Lord." The commentators read this as the reason the followers cannot be dismissed. Their standing is not determined by social rank but by the meeting that awaits them.

**The Social Logic the Verse Refuses**

The notables' request deserves examination, because it is not crude. They are not asking Noah to abandon his followers; they are asking him to separate them, so that the notables can associate with him without loss of status.

The commentators read the request as an expression of a social order in which association is ranked. To sit with the lowly is to become lowly, and the notables are unwilling to accept that consequence.

Noah's answer rejects the premise. The commentators note that he does not argue about the followers' worth or propose a separate gathering; he refuses the separation itself. The meeting with their Lord is what makes rank irrelevant.

The verse thus addresses a recurring problem in religious communities. The commentators connect it to the sūrah's broader concern with the notables, who appear in nearly every narrative as the ones who refuse.

The parallel at 6:52 is instructive, since there the Prophet is instructed not to drive away those who call upon their Lord morning and evening, seeking His face. The commentators read the two passages as making the same point from the prophet's side and God's.

**"I See That You Are an Ignorant People"**

The verse closes with *wa-lākinnī arākum qawman tajhalūn* — "but I see that you are an ignorant people." The commentators explain that this is in reference to the leaders' request that Noah should shun those who believed in God.

The ignorance named is specific. The commentators read it as ignorance of what makes a person valuable — the notables do not know what they are asking Noah to dismiss.

The verb *tajhalūn* is from the root *j-h-l*, which in Qur'anic usage carries the sense of acting without knowledge rather than merely lacking information. The commentators read it as describing a settled disposition.

Al-Ṭabarī notes that the identical phrase is used by the prophet Hūd when addressing his people, at 46:23. The commentators read the repetition as indicating a standard prophetic response to this particular refusal.

The verse ends on this note rather than on the refusal. The commentators read the placement as significant: Noah's final word to his people in this exchange is a diagnosis of their ignorance, not a restatement of his position.

**The Verse in the Noah Narrative**

Verse 29 sits within the exchange between Noah and the notables that runs from v. 27 to v. 34. The commentators read the sequence as a negotiation that fails because the terms offered are unacceptable.

The notables' position is stated at v. 27: we see you only as a human being like us, and we see only the lowest among us following you. Noah's answer at vv. 28–29 addresses both claims — he does not possess God's treasures, and he will not dismiss the followers.

The verse's refusal is what makes the subsequent destruction intelligible. The commentators read the narrative as showing that the notables were not merely unconvinced but unwilling to accept the conditions on which belief was offered.

The connection to the sūrah's opening is worth noting. Verse 7 described the trial as to which of you is most virtuous in deed. The notables' request would have made rank the criterion. Noah's refusal holds the verse's criterion in place.

The pastoral application the commentators draw is direct. The verse addresses any community in which association is ranked and the lowly believer is treated as an embarrassment. Noah's answer is given as the standing response.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:30

> **O my people! Who would help me against God, were I to drive them away? Will you not remember?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Who Would Help Me Against God" — The Question That Inverts the Notables' Frame**

Noah's third word to his people is a single question, and the question inverts the entire frame the notables have been operating in. *Man yanṣurunī min Allāh in ṣadartuhum* — "who would help me against God, were I to drive them away?" The notables had set a condition: dismiss the lowliest, and we will consider your message. Noah answers the condition by asking who would stand with him — *against God* — if he met it. The inversion is precise. In the notables' frame, the prophet is the one who needs *their* support; in the question's frame, the prophet is the one who would be *alone against God* if he yielded to them. The *sūrah* is stating, in the question, that the notables' condition is not a *negotiation*; it is a *trap*, and the *trap* is the sūrah's way of stating that *meeting the condition* would not *gain* the notables; it would *place* the prophet *outside* the *relation* that *sustains* him, and the *placement outside* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *not* *weak* because he *refuses*; he is *strong* because he *refuses*, and the *strength* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *refusal* is *not* a *loss*; it is a *preservation*.

**The Help That Is Asked For — and the One Who Is Not Asked**

The question is worth reading for what it does *not* ask. Noah does not ask, "who will help me *against you*?" — he does not cast the notables as the enemy. He asks, "who will help me *against God*?" — he casts *God* as the one against whom the *yielding* would stand. The *sūrah* is stating, in the formulation, that the prophet's *fear* is *not* the notables' *revenge*; it is the *relation* with God that would be *broken* by the *yielding*, and the *relation* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet's* *accountability* is *not* to the *people*; it is to the *Lord*, and the *accountability to the Lord* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *people* are *not* the *prophet's* *judges*; they are his *congregation*, and the *congregation* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* *speaks to them* but does *not* *answer to them*, and the *not answering to them* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *office* is *held* from *above*, not *elected* from *below*.

**"Were I to Drive Them Away" — The Hypothetical That Is Already Answered**

The *conditional* — *in ṣadartuhum*, "were I to drive them away" — is the sūrah's way of stating that the *act* is *not* a *real possibility* for the prophet; it is a *hypothetical* that the *question* *answers in advance*. The *sūrah* is stating, in the *conditional*, that the *prophet* has *already* *decided* not to *drive them away*, and that the *decision* is *not* a *preference*; it is a *consequence* of the *question*, and the *consequence* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *refusal* of verse 29's third clause is *not* a *sticking point*; it is a *principle*, and the *principle* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *not* *bargaining*; he is *standing*, and the *standing* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables'* *condition* is *not* a *term*; it is a *test*, and the *test* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *being tried* in the *same* *way* the *Makkans'* *Prophet* is *being tried* in *verse 12*, and the *sameness* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *consolation* of the *narrative middle* is *real*.

**"Will You Not Remember?" — The Address That Closes Noah's Second Word**

The verse closes with the same address that closed the sūrah's opening section in verse 24: *afalā tadhakkarūn* — "will you not remember?" The *recurrence* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* are *not* *without* the *capacity* to *remember*; they *have* the *capacity*, and the *capacity* is *not* *engaged*, and the *not being engaged* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *jāhil* of verse 29 is *not* a *lack*; it is a *suspension*, and the *suspension* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *knowledge* is *available* and *held back*, and the *holding back* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *account* is *not* *open* to the *notables*; it is *closed by their choice*, and the *closing by choice* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *verdict* of verse 22 — the *greatest of losers* — is *already* in *operation* in *Noah's* *notables*, and that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *verdict* *applied* to *people who are not them*, and that the *not being them* is the *last* *degree* of *distance* the *sūrah* will *allow* the *Makkans* to *maintain*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:31

> **I say not unto you that with me are the treasuries of God; nor do I know the Unseen. And I say not that I am an angel; nor do I say of those who are despicable in your eyes, 'God will not give them any good'—God knows best what is in their souls—for then I would indeed be among the wrongdoers."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"I Say Not" — The Verse Built on Four Denials**

Noah's fourth word to his people is built, almost entirely, on the formula *lā aqūl* — "I say not" — repeated four times, and the repetition is the sūrah's way of stating that the prophet's *position* is defined by what he *does not claim*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *structure*, that the *prophet's* *character* is *not* a *character* of *additions*; it is a *character* of *subtractions*, and the *subtractions* are the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* *removes* from *himself* everything that the *notables* *project* onto him, and the *removal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* the *person* the *notables* *require*; he is the *person* the *message* *requires*, and the *difference* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables'* *requirement* is a *requirement* that the *message* *cannot meet*.

**"With Me Are the Treasuries of God" — The First Denial, and the Wealth the Prophets Did Not Claim**

The first denial removes the *treasuries*: *ʿindī kanzāt Allāh* — "with me are the treasuries of God." The *kanz* — treasury — is the *word* that names the *stored wealth*, and the *denial* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *in* the *economy* of *stored wealth*, in the *sense of being* *without* the *treasury*, in the *sense of being* *outside* the *accumulation*. The *sūrah* is *answering*, in the *denial*, the *notables'* *frame* of *verse 29* — the *frame* in which *status* is *wealth* and *wealth* is *standing* — by *stating* that the *prophet's* *standing* is *not* *wealth*, and the *not being wealth* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *competing* with the *notables* in the *notables'* *arena*, and the *not competing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* a *rival*; he is a *witness*, and the *witness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *treasuries* are *God's*, not the *prophet's*, and that the *notables'* *search* for a *messenger* with *treasuries* is a *search* for a *merchant*, not a *prophet*.

**"Nor Do I Know the Unseen" — The Second Denial, and the Humility of the Channel**

The second denial removes the *Unseen*: *wa-lā aʿlamu-l-ghayb* — "nor do I know the Unseen." The *ghayb* — Unseen — is the *realm* that is *reserved* for *God* alone in the *Qur'an's* *theology*, and the *prophet's* *denial* of *knowledge* of it is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *beyond* the *human*, in the *sense of being* *within* the *human* *limits*, in the *sense of being* *the one who does not claim what belongs to God*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *denial*, that the *prophet's* *humility* is *not* a *weakness*; it is the *condition* of the *office*, and the *condition* is the *sūrah's way of stating* that the *one* who *claims* the *Unseen* is *not* a *prophet*; he is the *one* the *sūrah* *described* in *verse 18* — the *one* who *fabricates a lie against God* — and the *fabrication* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *boundary* between the *prophet* and the *fabricator* is *precise*: the *prophet* *denies* the *Unseen*, and the *fabricator* *claims* it, and the *denial* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *on the right side of the boundary*.

**"I Say Not That I Am an Angel" — The Third Denial, and the Humanity Refused as a Disqualification**

The third denial removes the *angelhood*: *wa-lā aqūlu annī malak* — "I say not that I am an angel." The *denial* is the *direct* *answer* to the *notables'* *first* *objection* of *verse 27* — the *objection* that the *prophet* is *merely* *human* — and the *answer* is *not* a *defense* of the *humanity*; it is an *acceptance* of it. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *denial*, that the *prophet* is *not* *claiming* to be *above* the *human*; he is *claiming* to be *human* and *sent*, and the *sent* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *humanity* is *not* a *disqualification*; it is the *form* of the *mission*, and the *form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables'* *objection* is *not* an *objection* to the *humanity*; it is an *objection* to the *possibility* of the *divine* *speaking* *through* the *human*, and the *impossibility* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables'* *epistemology* is *closed* in the *way* *described* in *verse 27*.

**"Nor Do I Say of the Despicable, 'God Will Not Give Them Good'" — The Fourth Denial, and the Justice the Prophets Kept**

The fourth denial is the one that answers the *notables'* *second* *objection* — the *objection* about the *lowliest* *followers* — and it is the *sūrah's* most *important* *denial*: *wa-lā aqūlu li-ṣ-ṣāfiḥīn fī aʿinyukum lā yaṭīlu-kum Allāhu khayrā* — "nor do I say of those who are despicable in your eyes, 'God will not give them any good.'" The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *denial*, that the *prophet* is *not* *the one* who *values* the *people* by the *notables'* *measure*, and that the *notables'* *measure* — the *measure* that *reads* the *lowliest* as *without* *good* — is *not* the *measure* the *prophet* *uses*, because the *measure* the *prophet* *uses* is *God's*, and *God's* *measure* *knows* what is in the *souls* — *wa-Allāhu aʿlamu bīmā fī anfusikum* — "God knows best what is in their souls." The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *clause*, that the *notables'* *valuation* is a *valuation of the surface*, and that *God's* *valuation* is a *valuation of the soul*, and that the *two* are *not* the *same*, and that the *prophet* *follows* *God's*, and the *following* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *lowliest* are *not* *without* *good* in the *measure* that *counts*, and that the *notables'* *contempt* is *not* a *judgment*; it is a *projection*, and the *projection* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables* are *judging* the *souls* they *cannot see*, and the *judging of the unseen* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables* are *in* the *state* of *verse 18* — *fabricating* — in the *realm* of the *souls*, and the *fabricating in the realm of the souls* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *final clause* — *fa-innī idhāan min aẓ-ẓālimīn*, "for then I would indeed be among the wrongdoers" — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that to *adopt* the *notables'* *measure* would *be* the *wrongdoing*, and the *wrongdoing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *spare*; he is *held*, by the *account*, to the *measure* of *God*, and the *holding* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *justice* the *prophet* *kept* is the *justice* the *sūrah* is *asking* the *Makkans* — who *despised* the *believers* of *low* *status* in the *same* *way* — to *see*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:32

> **They said, "O Noah! You have disputed with us, and have prolonged the disputation; so bring upon us that wherewith you have threatened us, if you are among the truthful."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"You Have Disputed with Us, and Have Prolonged the Disputation" — The Reframe That the Sūrah Records**

The notables' next word reframes the entire history of the encounter. Noah has, in the sūrah's telling, presented the proof (v. 28), denied the notables' conditions (vv. 29–31), and stood on the measure of God. The notables' summary of all of it is: *qād qāṣartan fī manaẓaratnā* — "you have prolonged the disputation with us." The *reframe* is the sūrah's way of stating that the notables do not experience the encounter as a *message presented and refused*; they experience it as a *dispute dragged out*, and the *dragging out* is the sūrah's way of stating that the notables have *reclassified* the *prophet's* *persistence* from *duty* into *annoyance*, and the *reclassification* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* are *not* *engaging* with the *content*; they are *managing* the *relation*, and the *managing of the relation* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* treat the *prophet* as a *nuisance to be ended*, not a *message to be considered*.

**"So Bring Upon Us That Wherewith You Have Threatened Us" — The Challenge That the Sūrah Will Hear Again**

The substance of the notables' word is the *challenge*: *fa-ātina bi-mā taʿuddu ʿalaynā in kunta min al-ṣādiqīn* — "so bring upon us that wherewith you have threatened us, if you are among the truthful." The *challenge* is to *produce* the *punishment* *on demand*, in the *sense of being* the *one* who *makes the threat* come *when* the *challenger* *wishes*, in the *sense of being* the *one* who *controls* the *timing* of the *divine* *act*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *challenge* is *not* a *request for proof*; it is a *request for control*, and the *request for control* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables* are *not* *doubting* the *warning*; they are *demanding* that the *warning* be *demonstrated* on *their* *terms*, and the *terms* are the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables* *want* the *punishment* to *be* *theirs to call*, because *calling it and having it not come* would be the *proof* of the *prophet's* *failure*, and the *proof of the failure* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *challenge* is *not* a *test of the prophet*; it is a *trap for the prophet*, and the *trap* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *notables* are *not* *seeking* the *truth*; they are *seeking* the *prophet's* *discredit*.

**"If You Are Among the Truthful" — The Condition That Reveals the Notables' Standard**

The *condition* — *in kunta min aṣ-ṣādiqīn*, "if you are among the truthful" — is the *sūrah's* window into the *notables'* *standard* of *truthfulness*. In the *notables'* *standard*, the *truthful* one is the *one* who *delivers* the *threatened* *punishment* *on demand*, and the *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *recording*, that the *standard* is *not* the *standard* of the *truth*; it is the *standard* of the *power*, in the *sense of being* the *standard* that *equates* the *truth* with the *capacity to compel*, in the *sense of being* the *standard* that *makes the prophet's truth depend on his ability to perform on the notables' schedule*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *clause*, that the *notables'* *standard* is *the* *standard* of *verse 12* — the *standard* that *demanded* a *treasure* *sent down* or an *angel* *coming with* the *Prophet* — and the *sameness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans'* *challenge* of *verse 12* is *not* a *new* *challenge*; it is the *challenge* of *Noah's* *notables*, *repeated*, and the *repetition* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* is *invariant* across *time*.

**The Challenge as the Notables' Final Position**

The sūrah records the challenge as the notables' *final* word before the prophet's *final* answer, and the *placement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *dialogue* has *reached* its *end*, in the *sense of being* the *point* at which the *notables* have *exhausted* their *reframes*, in the *sense of being* the *point* at which the *notables* have *moved* from *objection* to *defiance*, in the *sense of being* the *point* at which the *notables* are *no longer* *negotiating*; they are *demanding*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *placement*, that the *notables* have *crossed* the *line* from *refusal* to *defiance*, and that the *crossing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *now* *open* in the *way* *described* in *verse 8* — the *way* in which the *mocked* *will* *beset* — and that the *next* *verse* will be the *prophet's* *answer* to the *crossing*, and that the *answer* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* does not *meet* the *challenge*; he *removes* the *challenge* from the *arena* in which the *notables* *set* it.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:33

> **He said, "God alone shall bring it to you, if He will, and you cannot thwart [it]."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"God Alone Shall Bring It" — The Answer That Removes the Challenge from the Prophet's Hands**

Noah's answer to the challenge is a single statement that performs one precise act: it *removes* the punishment *from the prophet's hands* and *places* it *in God's*. *Innamā yāʾī bikum Allāh in shāʾ* — "God alone shall bring it to you, if He wills." The notables had demanded that the *prophet* produce the punishment *on demand*; the *answer* is that the *prophet* does not *produce* it, and *cannot* produce it, and that the *production* is *God's* alone, in the sense of being an act that the prophet neither initiates nor times nor controls. The sūrah is stating, in the answer, that the *challenge* was *set* in the *wrong arena*: the notables challenged the *prophet* to perform, and the *answer* moves the performance to the *One who performs*, and the *moving* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet's office* does not include the *performance of the punishment*, and that the *not including* is not a *limitation* of the office; it is its *definition*.

**"If He Will" — The Condition That the Sūrah Places on the Divine Act**

The *condition* — *in shāʾ*, "if He wills" — is the clause that the commentators have discussed at length, and the discussion is the sūrah's way of stating that the *divine act* is *not* a *compulsion* in the sense of being *unconditional*, and that the *conditioning* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *will* of God is the *ground* of the *act*, and that the *act* follows the *will*, not the other way around. The sūrah is stating, in the condition, that the *punishment* is *not* an *inevitability* in the sense of being *mechanical*; it is a *consequence* in the sense of being *willed*, and the *willed* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *account* is *not* a *machine*; it is a *decision*, and the *decision* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mercy* is *open* in the sense of being *the condition that holds the punishment in abeyance*, and that the *abeyance* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *reprieve* of verse 8 is *real*, and that the *reprieve* is *held* by the *will*, and that the *will* is *not* *bound* by the *notables' schedule*.

**"You Cannot Thwart It" — The Clause That Answers the Challenge's Real Aim**

The second half of the answer is the clause that meets the challenge's real aim: *wa-lā tinqidū minhu* — "and you cannot thwart it." The notables' *challenge* was *not* really about *producing* the punishment; it was about *demonstrating* that the punishment *could be thwarted*, in the sense of being shown that the prophet *could not* make it come *when they demanded*, and therefore *could not* make it come *at all*. The *answer* *reverses* the *demonstration*: the *notables* *cannot* thwart *it*, in the sense of being *unable* to *prevent* the *coming*, in the sense of being *unable* to *delay* the *coming*, in the sense of being *unable* to *survive* the *coming*. The sūrah is stating, in the clause, that the *power* the notables *believed* they *held* — the *power* to *demand* and *not* *receive*, to *mock* and *not* *beset* — is *not* a *power*; it is a *belief*, and the *belief* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* are *in* the *state* of *verse 20* — the *state* of *not* *being able to thwart* — and that the *state* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *Makkans'* *power* is the *same* *power* the *notables* *believed* they *held*, and that the *holding* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mirror* is *complete* in the *way* *described* in *verse 24*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:34

> **And my sincere advice will not benefit you, much as I desire to advise you, if God desires to cause you to err. He is your Lord, and unto Him shall you be returned."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"My Sincere Advice" — The Word That Names the Prophet's Remaining Function**

The verse opens with the word that names what the prophet *still* does after the challenge has been removed from his hands: *nāṣiḥah* — "sincere advice." The *nāṣiḥah* is not the *warning* of verse 25; it is the *advice*, in the sense of being the *counsel* of the *one who loves*, in the sense of being the *speech* of the *one who desires the good of the addressee*. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the prophet's *function* does not *end* with the *challenge*; it *continues* in the *form* of *advice*, and the *advice* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *not* *finished* with the *people*; he is *still* *speaking* to them, and the *still speaking* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *office* is *not* a *one-time* *act*; it is a *continuing* *relation*, and the *relation* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* *loves* the *people* in the *sense* of *desiring their good*, and that the *desiring* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *fear* of verse 26 is *not* *exhausted*; it is *continuing*.

**"If God Desires to Cause You to Err" — The Condition That the Sūrah States with Care**

The *condition* — *in yurīd Allāh an yuḍillakum* — "if God desires to cause you to err" — is the clause that the sūrah states with the *care* the commentators have noted, and the care is the sūrah's way of stating that the *causing to err* is *not* an *arbitrary* *act*; it is a *consequence* in the *sense* of being the *result* of the *notables'* *own* *refusal*, in the *sense* of being the *sealing* of the *state* the *notables* *have chosen*. The sūrah is stating, in the clause, that the *notables'* *refusal* — the *refusal* of verses 27–32, the *refusal* that *moved* from *objection* to *defiance* — has *reached* the *point* at which the *state* is *sealed*, and that the *sealing* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *advice* is *no longer* *effective*, not because the *advice* is *weak*, but because the *receiver* is *closed*. The sūrah is stating, in the clause, that the *prophet's* *advice* is *not* a *magic* that *works* regardless of the *receiver*; it is a *counsel* that *requires* the *receiving*, and the *not receiving* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* are *in* the *state* of verse 20 — the *state* of *not being able to hear* — and that the *state* is *the reason* the *advice* *does not benefit*, and that the *reason* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *account* is *just*, because the *one* who is *not benefited* is the *one* who *did not receive*, and the *not receiving* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *verdict* is *not* an *imposition*; it is a *consequence*.

**"Much as I Desire to Advise You" — The Prophet's Will, Stated**

The *clause* — *wa-law ashtahaytu ihdāyakum lā tanfaʿakum nāṣiḥatī* — "much as I desire to advise you" — states the *prophet's* *will*, and the *stating* is the sūrah's way of *clearing* the *prophet* of the *charge* that the *advice* *failed* because the *prophet* *did not try*. The sūrah is stating, in the clause, that the *prophet* *did* *desire*, in the sense of *willing* the *good* of the *people*, in the sense of *aiming* the *advice* at the *receiving*, in the sense of *doing* everything that the *office* *requires*. The sūrah is stating, in the clause, that the *failure* of the *advice* is *not* a *failure* of the *advice*; it is a *failure* of the *reception*, and the *failure of the reception* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *not* *responsible* for the *reception*, and that the *not being responsible* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *office* is *complete* in the sense of being *done* when the *advice* is *given*, and that the *given* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* has *done* his *part*, and that the *part* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *account* is *his* in the sense of being *his to render*, and that the *rendering* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *not* *burdened* with the *people's* *fate*; he is *burdened* with the *message*, and the *message* is *delivered*.

**"He Is Your Lord, and Unto Him Shall You Be Returned" — The Closing That Restores the Frame**

The verse closes by *restoring* the frame that the notables had *displaced*: *huwa rabbukum wa-ilyhi al-maʿād* — "He is your Lord, and unto Him shall you be returned." The *restoration* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables'* *entire* *encounter* — the *objections*, the *conditions*, the *challenge* — has *taken place* inside a *frame* that the *notables* *refused*, and that the *frame* is the *sūrah's* way of stating that the *Lord* is *the one* to *whom* the *return* is, and that the *return* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables'* *present* *order* is *not* the *whole* of the *time*, in the *way* *described* in verse 29's *warrant* — the *warrant* that the *believers* *shall meet their Lord*. The sūrah is closing Noah's speech with the *return*, and the *closing* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *encounter* is *over*, and that the *next* *section* of the *sūrah* — the *section* that *begins* with the *accusation* of *fabrication* in *verse 35* and *continues* with the *revelation* to *Noah* in *verse 36* — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *story* is *not* *finished* by the *encounter*; it is *finished* by the *flood*, and that the *flood* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *return* is *real*, and that the *real* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *return* *stated* to *people who are not them*, and that the *not being them* is the *last* *degree* of *distance* the *sūrah* will *allow*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:35

> **Or do they say, "He has fabricated it"? Say, "If I have fabricated it, then my guilt is upon me; but I am quit of that which you commit."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Sūrah Returns to the Accusation — and Why It Returns**

The sūrah, in the middle of the Noah narrative, turns back to the Makkans with the same accusation it answered in verse 13: *am yaqūlūna kafaahu* — "or do they say, 'he has fabricated it'?" The return is not a repetition; it is the sūrah's way of stating that the *accusation* is the *Makkans'* *standing position*, and that the *position* *survives* the *answers*, in the sense of being *renewed* after each *refutation*, in the sense of being *the position that does not depend on the answers*. The sūrah is stating, in the return, that the *accusation* is not an *argument* the Makkans *make*; it is a *stance* the Makkans *hold*, and the *holding* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *answers* of verse 13 are *not* *exhaustive* of the *accusation*, and that the *sūrah* will *return* to the *accusation* *again* in the *narrative middle*, in the sense of being the *theme* that the *stories* *demonstrate*.

**"If I Fabricated It, Then My Guilt Is Upon Me" — The Conditional Restated in the Narrative**

The *answer* is *restated* in the *same* *form* as in *verse 13*: *in kuzibtuhu fa-ʿalayya iḍbūḥī* — "if I have fabricated it, then my guilt is upon me." The *restatement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *answer* is *not* a *one-time* *response*; it is a *standing* *position*, and the *standing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* *does not* *vary* his *answer* to the *accusation*, in the *sense of being* *consistent*, in the *sense of being* *the one who holds the position regardless of the pressure*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *restatement*, that the *conditional* of *verse 13* is *the Prophet's* *permanent* *answer*, and that the *permanence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *not* *arguing*; he is *stating*, and the *stating* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *guilt* is *assigned* to the *fabricator*, and that the *assignment* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *accuser* is *not* *free* of the *accusation*, in the *sense of being *the one who bears the weight of the charge he makes*, and that the *bearing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* who *accuse* are *not* *without* a *stake* in the *accusation*, and that the *stake* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *accusation* is *not* a *neutral* *charge*; it is a *bet*, and the *bet* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *betting* their *own* *account* on the *falsehood* of the *Qur'an*, and that the *betting* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *in* the *state* of *verse 18* — the *state* of *fabricating a lie* — in the *sense of being* the *ones* who *compose* the *accusation* and *present* it as a *revelation* of the *truth* about the *Prophet*.

**"But I Am Quit of That Which You Commit" — The Disassociation Restated**

The *second* *half* of the *answer* is *restated* in the *same* *form*: *wa-anā min mā taʿmalūna muzāq* — "but I am quit of that which you commit." The *restatement* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *disassociation* is *not* a *response* to a *specific* *act*; it is a *standing* *position* of *separation* between the *Prophet's* *account* and the *Makkans'* *account*, and the *separation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *not* *in* the *Makkans'* *enterprise*, in the *sense of being* *outside* the *accusation*, in the *sense of being* *free* of the *charge*, in the *sense of being* *the one who does not share the guilt of the one who accuses falsely*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *restatement*, that the *Makkans'* *accusation* is *their* *act*, and that the *act* is *their* *account*, and that the *Prophet* is *quit* of the *account*, and that the *being quit* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *not* *defending* himself *against* the *Makkans*; he is *stating* the *separation*, and the *stating* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* of *verse 18* — the *account* in which the *fabricators* are *brought before their Lord* and the *witnesses* *testify* — is the *Makkans'* *account*, not the *Prophet's*, and that the *not being his* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *safe* in the *sense of being* *outside* the *charge*, and that the *safety* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *consolation* of the *narrative middle* is *real*: the *Prophet* is *not* *alone* in the *accusation*; *Noah's* *people* *made* the *same* *accusation* in the *same* *form*, and the *same form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *in company* across *time*, and that the *company* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *burden* is *shared*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:36

> **And it was revealed unto Noah, "None of thy people shall believe, save those who had already believed. So be not distressed by that which they used to do."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"None of Thy People Shall Believe, Save Those Who Had Already Believed" — The Revelation That Ends the Hope**

The sūrah now turns from the Makkans' accusation back to the Noah narrative, and it turns with a revelation that ends the last degree of the prophet's hope: *wa-ūḥiya ilā Nūḥin an lā yanfaʿa īmānan li-qaumin mā kānū yaʿmalūn ʾillā lladhīna āmanū qabl dhālika* — "none of thy people shall believe, save those who had already believed." The revelation is the sūrah's way of stating that the *encounter* described in verses 27–34 has *reached* its *end* in the *sense* of being *the point* at which the *conversion* is *closed*, and that the *closed* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *persistence* — the *persistence* the *notables* *called* *prolonging the disputation* — has *reached* the *limit* set by the *divine* *knowledge*, and that the *limit* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* did not *stop* too *early*; he *continued* until the *continuing* was *no longer* *effective*, and the *no longer effective* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *office* is *complete* in the *sense of being* *done* when the *divine* *knowledge* *says* it is *done*, and that the *done* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *abandoning* the *people*; he is *completing* the *mission*.

**The Long Calling — and the Sūrah's Way of Stating Its Duration**

The sūrah's statement of the closed hope carries, in the commentators' gloss, the duration of the calling. It is reported that Noah called his people for nine hundred and fifty years, and the Qur'an states in 29:14:

> *"And he remained among them a thousand years less fifty."*

The *duration* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *persistence* is *not* a *brief* *effort*; it is a *lifetime*, and the *lifetime* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *closed hope* is *not* a *closed hope after a week*; it is a *closed hope after a life*, and the *after a life* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *fear* of verse 26 — the *fear* for the *people* — was *held* for a *lifetime*, and that the *holding* is the *sūrah's way of stating* that the *love* is *not* a *momentary* *feeling*; it is a *lifetime* *posture*, and the *posture* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet's* *own* *persistence* in *Makkah* is *not* *unprecedented*; it is *the* *persistence* of *Noah*, and the *sameness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *consolation* of the *narrative middle* is *real* in the *way* *described* in *verse 120*.

**"Be Not Distressed by That Which They Used to Do" — The Counsel That the Sūrah Gives to the Prophet**

The *second* *clause* of the *revelation* is the *counsel*: *fa-lā taḥzan ʿalā mā kānū yaʿmalūn* — "so be not distressed by that which they used to do." The *counsel* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *distress* is *seen*, in the *sense of being* *known* to the *One* who *reveals*, and that the *seeing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *alone* in the *distress*, and that the *not being alone* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *counsel* is *not* a *command* to *stop feeling*; it is a *counsel* to *place* the *feeling* in the *frame* that *holds* it, and the *frame* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *account* is *not* the *people's* *fate*; it is the *message*, and the *message* is *delivered*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *counsel*, that the *prophet* is *not* *to carry* the *weight* of the *people's* *refusal*, because the *weight* is *not his to carry*, and the *not being his to carry* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *office* is *not a burden of outcome*; it is a *duty of delivery*, and the *duty* is *complete* when the *delivery* is *done*.

**The Counsel as the Sūrah's Standing Answer to the Prophet's Distress**

The sūrah's counsel to Noah is the sūrah's *standing answer* to the *Prophet's* *constrained breast* of *verse 12*, and the *sameness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *consolation* of the *narrative middle* is *not* a *general* *comfort*; it is a *specific* *counsel* given to a *specific* *prophet* in a *specific* *moment*, and that the *specificity* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *not* *imagining* his *distress*; it is *the distress of Noah*, and that the *distress of Noah* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *in company*, and that the *company* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *burden* is *shared*, and that the *sharing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Prophet* is *not* *alone* in the *Makkan* *siege*, and that the *not being alone* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *counsel* is *real*, and that the *real* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *next* *verse* — the *command* to *build* — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mission* is *not* *over* by the *closed hope*; it is *transformed* by it, and the *transformation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *finished*; he is *about to begin the ark*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:37

> **Build the Ark before Our Eyes and by Our Revelation. And address Me not concerning those who did wrong; surely they shall be drowned."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Build the Ark" — The Command That Transforms the Mission**

The closed hope of verse 36 is followed, without interval, by a command: *fa-ṣnaʿ al-safīna bi-aʿyadinā* — "build the ark." The *command* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mission* is *not* *over* by the *closed hope*; it is *transformed* by it, and the *transformation* is the *sūrah's way of stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *released* from the *people*; he is *given a new task* in *relation to them*, and the *new task* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *relation* to the *people* is *not* *ended* by the *closed hope*; it is *deepened*, and the *deepening* is the *sūrah's way of stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *alone* in the *building*; he is *building* in the *presence* of the *One* who *commanded*.

**"Before Our Eyes" — The Phrase That the Commentators Have Discussed**

The *phrase* — *bi-aʿyadinā wa-waḥyina*, "before Our eyes and by Our revelation" — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *building* is *not* a *private* *act*; it is a *witnessed* *act*, in the *sense of being* *performed* in the *presence* of the *divine* *witnessing*, in the *sense of being* *carried out* according to the *divine* *instruction*. The *commentators* *note* that the *aʿyad* — "eyes" — is a *figurative* *expression* for the *divine* *watching over*, and the *watching over* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *alone* in the *building*, in the *sense of being* *accompanied* by the *One* whose *eyes* are *on the work*, and that the *accompaniment* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *building* is *not* a *task*; it is a *communion*, and the *communion* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *building* a *ship*; he is *building* in the *presence* of the *One* who *sent* him, and the *presence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *consolation* of *verse 36* is *not* a *general* *comfort*; it is a *specific* *accompaniment*, and the *accompaniment* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *alone* in the *longest* *task* of his *life*.

**"By Our Revelation" — The Instruction That the Ark Was Given**

The *second* *half* of the *first* *clause* — *wa-waḥyina*, "by Our revelation" — is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ark* is *not* a *product* of *human* *design*; it is a *product* of *divine* *instruction*, and the *instruction* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *inventing*; he is *following*, and the *following* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *ark* is *not* a *ship* in the *sense of being* a *vessel designed by its builder*; it is a *vessel* in the *sense of being* a *form given by the One who sent the flood*, and the *given form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *flood* and the *ark* are *one* *act*, in the *sense of being* the *sending* and the *saving* of the *same* *divine* *will*, and that the *one act* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *punishment* and the *salvation* are *not* *two* *acts*; they are *one*, and the *one* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mercy* is *not* separate from the *judgment*; it is *the form of the judgment for the few*.

**"Address Me Not Concerning Those Who Did Wrong" — The Prohibition That the Sūrah States**

The *second* *command* is a *prohibition*: *wa-lā tukhṭin ilayya ʿalā-l-munkirīn* — "address me not concerning those who did wrong." The *prohibition* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *to intercede* for the *wrongdoers*, in the *sense of being* *the one who pleads with God to withhold the punishment from them*, in the *sense of being* the *one who asks for the reprieve to be extended*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *prohibition*, that the *prophet's* *fear* of *verse 26* — the *fear* for the *people* — has *reached* the *limit* at which the *fear* must *not* be *expressed* as *intercession*, and that the *limit* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet's* *love* is *not* a *ground* for the *withholding* of the *account*, and that the *not being a ground* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *just* in the *sense of being* *the same for all*, and that the *same for all* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *above* the *account*, and that the *not being above* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *office* does not *include* the *intercession for the wrongdoers in this life*, and that the *not including* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *held* to the *measure* of *God*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 31*.

**"Surely They Shall Be Drowned" — The Word That Closes the Command**

The *command* *closes* with the *word* that *names* the *fate*: *innahum masbūḥūn* — "surely they shall be drowned." The *masbūḥ* — drowned — is the *word* that the *sūrah* will *return* to in *verse 43*, in the *form* of the *son* *among the drowned*, and the *return* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *drowning* is *not* a *collective* *abstraction*; it is a *specific* *fate* for *specific* *persons*, and that the *specificity* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *not* a *general* *event*; it is a *personal* *fate*, and the *personal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *drowning* *stated* to *Noah's people*, and that the *stating* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mirror* is *complete*, and that the *complete* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *next* *verses* — the *building* and the *mocking* — are the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *story* is *not* *over* by the *command*; it is *in the building*, and that the *in the building* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* are *hearing* the *prophet* *work* while they *mock*, and that the *mocking* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *pattern* of *verse 8* — the *mocked* *will beset* — is *in operation* in *Noah's* *city*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:38

> **And he was building the Ark, and whenever notables among his people passed by him, they would scoff at him. He said, "If you scoff at us, surely we shall scoff at you, just as you scoff."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"He Was Building" — The Verb That Places the Prophet at Work**

The sūrah now places the prophet at work, and the placement is done with a single verb in the imperfect: *wa- huwa yaṣnaʿ al-safīna* — "and he was building the ark." The *imperfect* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *building* is *ongoing*, in the sense of being *in progress*, in the sense of being *the act that fills the prophet's days*. The sūrah is stating, in the verb, that the *mission* of the ark is not a single act of launching; it is a *course of work*, and the *course of work* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *engaged* in the *task* that the notables *mock*, and that the *engagement* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *not* waiting for the flood; he is *building for the flood*, and the *building for the flood* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *prophet* is *acting on the revelation* in the visible world, and that the *acting* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *revelation* of verse 37 is *not* a private knowledge; it is a *public work*, and the *public work* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* will *see* it, and that the *seeing* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* is *not* a private feeling; it is a *public act*, performed *passing by* the work.

**"Whenever Notables Passed by" — The Mockery as a Public Spectacle**

The sūrah specifies who mocks: *wa-inna al-malaʾ min qawmihi yaṣifūnahu* — "whenever notables among his people passed by him, they would scoff at him." The *notables* are the same notables of verse 27, and the *passing by* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* is *not* a sustained engagement; it is a *spectacle*, in the sense of being *performed* in *passing*, in the sense of being *the act of the one who does not stop to consider*, in the sense of being *the act of the one who takes the work as entertainment*. The sūrah is stating, in the specification, that the *notables* are *not* *arguing* with the prophet; they are *performing* at him, and the *performing* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* is *not* an objection; it is a *display*, and the *display* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* are *using* the *prophet's work* as the *object* of their *public* *identity*, and that the *using* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* is *not* about the ark; it is about the *notables'* *need* to *be seen* as the *ones who laugh at the prophet*, and the *need to be seen* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables* are *in* the *state* of *verses 15–16* — the *state* of *desiring the world and its adornment* — and that the *adornment* they *seek* is the *adornment of being the laughers*, and the *laughers* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* is *their* *deed*, and that the *deed* is *their* *account*.

**"If You Scoff at Us, We Shall Scoff at You" — The Promise That the Sūrah Will Fulfill in Its Own Text**

The prophet's answer is a *promise* structured as a *mirror*: *in taḥtanūna fa-innā naḥtani* — "if you scoff at us, surely we shall scoff at you, just as you scoff." The *mirror* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* is *not* a *one-way* act; it is a *round-trip*, in the sense of being *the act that returns to the actor*, and the *returning* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *law of verse 8* — the law that *the mocked will beset* — is *the specific form* in which the *mockery returns*. The sūrah is stating, in the promise, that the *believers* will *scoff* at the *disbelievers* in the *final state*, in the sense of being *the ones who see the outcome and know*, in the sense of being *the ones who laugh at the fate of the ones who laughed at them*, and the *final laughter* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* is *not* a *neither* act; it is a *temporal* act, and that the *temporal* act has a *second half*, and that the *second half* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *notables'* *laughter* is *not* the *last* laughter; it is the *first* laughter, and the *first* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *last* belongs to the *builders*, and that the *belonging* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *pattern* of 83:29–36 — the pattern in which the disbelievers laugh at the believers and the believers laugh at the disbelievers — is *the sūrah's standing description of the two halves of the mockery*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:39

> **For soon you shall know upon whom there comes a punishment that disgraces him and upon whom there falls a punishment enduring."**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Soon You Shall Know" — The Temporal Word That the Sūrah Uses with Precision**

The verse closes Noah's word to the mockers with a temporal promise: *qarīban taʿlamūna* — "soon you shall know." The *qarīb* — soon, near — is the word the sūrah uses throughout for the *proximity* of the *account*, and the *use* here is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mockers'* *certainty* — the *certainty* that the *ark* is a *jest*, that the *flood* is a *rumor*, that the *prophet* is a *laughingstock* — is *not* a *certainty* that *holds*; it is a *certainty* that is *near* to its *end*, and the *nearness* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *knowledge* the *mockers* *lack* is *coming*, and that the *coming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *flood* is *not* a *distant* *event*; it is *near*, in the *sense of being* *within the time of the builders and the mockers alike*, and that the *within the time* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mockers* are *not* *safe* by *distance*; they are *in* the *range* of the *coming*.

**"A Punishment That Disgraces Him" — The First Punishment, and the Disgrace It Carries**

The *first* of the *two* *punishments* named is *ʿadhāb munkhī* — "a punishment that disgraces him." The *inkhāʾ* — disgrace, humiliation — is the word that names the *quality* of the *first* punishment, and the *quality* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *first* punishment is *not* merely *destructive*; it is *humiliating*, in the sense of being *the act that exposes*, in the sense of being *the act that makes the hidden public*, in the sense of being *the act that turns the mockers' performance of verse 38 into the spectacle they will be in*. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *flood* — the *punishment* the *mockers* *laughed at* — will *disgrace* them, in the *sense of being* *the water that carries them away in the form of the ones who could not save themselves*, in the *sense of being* *the event that makes their laughter the object of the account*. The *disgrace* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *mockery* of *verse 38* is *not* a *private* *act*; it is a *public* *act*, and that the *public* act is *met* by a *public* *disgrace*, and that the *public disgrace* is the sūrah's way of stating that the *law of verse 8* — the law that *the mocked will beset* — is *the specific form* in which the *disgrace* is *carried out*.

**"A Punishment Enduring" — The Second Punishment, and the Duration It Carries**

The *second* of the *two* punishments is *ʿadhāb baqī* — "a punishment enduring." The *bqāʾ* — endurance, duration — is the word that names the *quality* of the *second* punishment, and the *quality* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *second* punishment is *not* a *worldly* event; it is the *punishment of the Hereafter*, in the sense of being *the punishment that does not end*, in the sense of being *the punishment that outlasts the first*, in the sense of being *the punishment that the first is a figure for*. The sūrah is stating, in the word, that the *two punishments* are *not* the *same* punishment described twice; they are *two* punishments, and that the *first* is *the worldly* disgrace — the flood, the drowning, the exposure — and that the *second* is *the enduring* — the fire, the account, the permanence — and that the *two together* form the *sūrah's* complete *description* of the *fate* of the *mockers*, and that the *complete description* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mockers'* *fate* is *not* a *single* event; it is a *sequence*, and that the *sequence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *worldly* disgrace is *not* the *end*; it is the *beginning* of the *enduring*, and that the *beginning of the enduring* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Makkans* who *mock* the *Prophet* are *in* the *same* *sequence*, and that the *in the same sequence* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mirror* is *complete* in the *way* *described* in *verse 24*.

---
## Sūrah Hūd 11:40

> **Till when Our Command came, and the oven gushed forth, We said, "Carry on board two of every kind, and thy family—save the one against whom the Word has already gone forth—and those who believe." And none believed along with him, save a few.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Till When Our Command Came" — The Temporal Word That Closes the Building**

The sūrah moves from the building to the coming with a single temporal word: *ḥattā idhā jāʾa amruna* — "till when Our command came." The *ḥattā* — "till" — is the sūrah's way of stating that the *building* had a *duration*, and that the *duration* was *closed* by the *coming*, and that the *coming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *command* of verse 37 — the *command* to *build* — is *not* a *command* that *stands*; it is a *command* that *leads*, and that the *leading* is to the *coming*, and that the *coming* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *divine* *act* is *not* a *process* that the *prophet* *manages*; it is a *command* that *comes*, in the *sense of being* *the act that arrives at its appointed time*, and that the *appointed time* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *qarīb* of verse 39 — the *soon* — has *arrived*, and that the *arrived* is the *sūrah's way of stating* that the *mockers'* *certainty* of *verse 38* is *ended*, and that the *ended* is the *sūrah's way of stating* that the *knowledge* the *mockers* *lacked* is *now* the *knowledge* of *all*, in the *sense of being* *the knowledge of the water*.

**"The Oven Gushed Forth" — The Phrase That the Commentators Have Identified**

The *second* element of the *coming* is *wa-faḍara-l-fūr* — "and the oven gushed forth." The *fūr* — oven, furnace — is the word that the commentators identify as the *earth* itself, in the sense of being *the earth that gushes forth water as an oven gushes forth heat and smoke*, and the *identification* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *flood* is *not* a *rain* in the *ordinary* sense; it is a *gushing* in the *sense of being* *the earth itself producing the water*, in the *sense of being* *the ground opening into a fountain*, in the *sense of being* *the solid becoming the liquid*. The sūrah is stating, in the phrase, that the *flood* is *not* a *weather event*; it is a *creation event*, in the *sense of being* *the earth acting in a way it has not acted before*, in the *sense of being* *the ground doing what the sky does*, and that the *doing* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *punishment* is *not* a *natural* *disaster*; it is a *divine* *act* in the *sense of being* *the act of the One who made the earth and the sky*, and that the *act* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mockers* are *not* *facing* a *storm*; they are *facing* the *command*, and that the *facing the command* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mūnaʿ* of verse 8 — the *not being turned away* — is *in operation*, in the *sense of being* *the water that does not stop*.

**"Carry on Board Two of Every Kind" — The Command That Preserves the Species**

The *divine* *speech* within the *verse* is the *command* to *Noah*: *wa-ḥamil fīhā min kulli zawjayni ithnayn* — "carry on board two of every kind." The *command* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *flood* is *not* a *total* *destruction*; it is a *destruction* with a *preservation*, and that the *preservation* is *not an accident*; it is a *command*, in the *sense of being* *the act of the One who sends the flood and saves the pairs*, and that the *pairs* — the *two of every kind* — are the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *life* on the *earth* is *not* *ended* by the *flood*; it is *carried* through it, in the *sense of being* *the seed of the next world*, in the *sense of being* *the continuation of the creation after the judgment*, and that the *continuation* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *mercy* is *not* separate from the *judgment*; it is *the form of the judgment for the preserved*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 37*.

**"Thy Family—Save the One Against Whom the Word Has Already Gone Forth" — The Exception Named**

The *second* element of the *command* is the *family*, with the *exception*: *wa-aḥlaka illā man saaba ʿalayhi qawl* — "and thy family—save the one against whom the word has already gone forth." The *exception* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *family* is *not* a *collective* *preservation*; it is a *family* with a *specific* *member* *excluded*, and that the *exclusion* is *not* a *later* *addition*; it is *within* the *command*, in the *sense of being* *the form of the command itself*. The *sūrah* is *stating*, in the *exception*, that the *salvation* is *not* by *blood*; it is by *faith*, in the *sense of being* *the salvation that follows the belief rather than the kinship*, and that the *following the belief* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *son* who *will* *drown* in *verses 42–43* is *not* *excluded* by the *flood*; he is *excluded* by the *word that has gone forth against him*, and that the *word* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *account* is *not* a *family* *account*; it is a *personal* *account*, and that the *personal* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *kinship* does not *carry* the *account*, and that the *not carrying* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *Noah* who *will* *plead* for the *son* in *verse 45* is *pleading* against the *form of the command*, and that the *pleading against the form* is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *next* *verses* are *the sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *prophet* is *not* *above* the *command*, in the *way* *described* in *verse 37's prohibition*.

**"And None Believed Along with Him, Save a Few" — The Closing That States the Number**

The *verse* *closes* with the *statement* of the *number*: *wa-mā āmana maʿahu illā qalīl* — "and none believed along with him, save a few." The *qalīl* — few — is the *word* that the *sūrah* *uses* for the *believers* *throughout* the *narrative middle*, and the *use* here is the *sūrah's* way of *stating* that the *few* is *not* a *disappointment*; it is the *form* of the *salvation*, in the *sense of being* *the salvation that is few in number and complete in quality*, in the *sense of being* *the salvation that is the seed of the next world*, in the *sense of being* *the salvation that carries the species and the message together*. The sūrah is stating, in the closing, that the *mockers* of verse 38 *are not the majority*; they are *the many who did not believe*, and that the *many who did not believe* are *the ones who will be drowned*, and that the *drowning of the many* is *the sūrah's way of stating* that the *few* are *the ones who will carry the next world*, and that the *carrying* is *the sūrah's way of stating* that the *Makkans* who *mock the Prophet* are *the many*, and that the *believers* who *follow him* are *the few*, and that the *few* are *the ones who will carry the next world*, and that the *carrying* is *the sūrah's way of stating* that the *mirror is complete*.
